## External engine integration

**Development installation clarification, 4 November:** The installed development launcher SHA-256 `b72b7c9eb5445b4620a890c5a6573942c64604d70520a5767fa1e46c6083943b` contains the external-handoff and native-Wine-bridge symbols. Its recorded app source tree `cd6cc1e52e8214c23df72cbfe24dda582a449b01` connects the provider overview to engine settings and the handoff and bridge sheets to game actions. Earlier source-review evidence saying these slices were not installed predates this development installation. Inclusion is not evidence that an external provider, Xbox runtime, renderer, game launch or gameplay is qualified.

Source presence, external handoff, licensed Xbox runtime admission and gameplay remain separate outcomes.

**REQ-178/179/182/187 and REQ-197/198 composition:** A scratch composition applies the bridge patch over the separately reviewed Settings and authenticated-harness candidate. Their changed paths are disjoint, all 29 checkout preimages match, and both sets of target bytes and modes are retained. The combined patch SHA-256 is `637c29e11ed931fdf3719c43ac620f9a8517702b8ee0289d11aefeccee81547d`, 371-file manifest `3bddbbd165d2f449ad2bc44819ca097c94210f45b5103d54dd68af1d99fb9846`, and archive `d28d02607d68ca7178065f33be8cdb203e2f5bfb648cfd536370f496e2774e94`. **REQ-178/179/182/187 source review:** `review: 88357567-6f67-4579-a993-1a271f2b263a REVISE` for the bridge port. The reviewer verified the 13-path patch and all 357 target files, and found the external-handoff code identical to its reviewed lineage. The Highball bridge relies on a pinned export's historical `observedAcceptedLicenses` rather than verifying the provider's current licence state or supported acceptance flow, contrary to C-16. The Porting Kit native bridge required by C-19 is also absent; no supported Porting Kit runtime was found, so this is an unresolved requirement rather than an observed false claim. Neither gap permits treating an external handoff as a licensed Xbox launch. The bridge remains unapplied.

**REQ-187 bridge correction candidate:** A separate scratch revision checks the current Highball configuration and active engine manifest against the Apple GPTK acceptance record and pinned manifest fields, without invoking or mutating the provider. The producer reports that the Porting Kit native bridge still lacks a supported selected-runtime closure and invocation contract; C-19 remains open. The 13-path patch SHA-256 is `c6539f4d0d5a3d3563d6fddac3e012f1ef6b44fe65ab66673866e8ff08c4f5b8`, manifest `b1bc302f6557c25658f815cef28cee37a74c65244bdd2106508d6ff3acbf4fb7`, and target tree `3f8ec0dc836afe46e84a0365e8c257fb727850d0`. No build, product action or independent re-review has yet been reported for this revision.

**REQ-187 correction review:** `review: 88357567-6f67-4579-a993-1a271f2b263a REVISE`. The candidate reads the default or configured store, but does not bind that store to the effective `HIGHBALL_HOME` of the selected provider process or to the tooling consuming the export. An accepted default store can therefore be mistaken for the provider's current store. The reviewer corrected an earlier scoped PASS: inability to observe an override does not prove that none exists. The reviewed source remains unapplied; the unimplemented Porting Kit path also remains open.

**REQ-187 fail-closed revision:** The next scratch revision refuses Highball native selection before runtime inspection or launch because its effective provider store and current licence acceptance cannot be bound. Existing checks were adjusted to assert that refusal; the producer did not build or run them. Patch SHA-256 `307f424258c475f1aa0ceddde91b524653da63c57f86653b2610475e54336537`, reviewed manifest `cea4f52b6066db5a6e633194bfc18097af6c4d61e63691bac5137b28ed94a62c`, target tree `f20882bde970b3270edd8a73fee9cf9177d2f552`. The producer later recorded the review in manifest `4d4228266b4284e3b70e085c32ee4132353454a37d5d81dc7655ab7c8358030e`; patch and target tree are unchanged. `review: 88357567-6f67-4579-a993-1a271f2b263a PASS` is limited to this fail-closed source boundary; it is not acceptance of Highball support, Porting Kit, runtime behavior or gameplay. Both native provider gaps remain open.

**REQ-185/187 source composition and copy:** `review: 3c4c9404-c34f-4bfc-a93d-d1e0bef86176 PASS` for the 372-file account-plus-provider composition using the fail-closed revision, with no build or runtime acceptance. A separate copy-only follow-up makes the diagnostic intro and picker name Heroic rather than offering unavailable Highball: patch SHA-256 `62ce2a568a0a898264f29091a9c52bdb1a239f259bc744b063af3d8faff29e25`, manifest `4a5027ced0efd3568af5443c1f13d7a8435aaf98ee527c665d50913a50c18696`, target tree `29fb23a1d7491f4ae59bf0d3c7368ad2fce51934`. `review: 88357567-6f67-4579-a993-1a271f2b263a PASS` verified only those two copy changes and unchanged safety code. A final composed-source review and focused execution remain pending.

**REQ-178/179/182/187 provider source integration:** The revised location, account and provider composition received the source review recorded under REQ-184/185/187. The full-contract candidate then passed six existing external-handoff tests and both nonshipping and shipping builds; its separate engine selector exposed the REQ-193 conflict rather than passing. After the 365 current-checkout source preimages and six absent paths were checked, the reviewed 13-path provider patch was applied. All 371 source paths outside this ledger matched the tested contract candidate, including modes. This integrates external-handoff and fail-closed diagnostic source only: real handoffs, Highball native selection, a Porting Kit runtime, licensed Xbox launch and gameplay remain unverified.

**REQ-197/198 and bridge structural composition review:** The same reviewer returned `PASS` for source reconstruction only: the 29-path patch reproduces all 371 recorded files and modes and preserves both disjoint slices. This does not override the bridge's REVISE verdict or establish a runtime outcome. The separately reviewed REQ-197/198 slice was subsequently integrated as recorded below; the bridge slice was not.

## Original issue work in the older checkout

Both were closed by merged PR `15001d57a45fdada72b6f9d13dd18872fa6ce443`, whose first parent is the user checkout's current HEAD. The checkout has later uncommitted changes but lacks the merged scenario and Settings test sources. Merged status is not evidence that the current checkout contains those changes.

**REQ-197/198 candidate:** The merged issue work is composed in scratch over the current reviewed source, including the newly integrated release port. The 16-path patch SHA-256 is `00f8b5d2ed3bb78c4e2a1d420db73c32d086d9f2d76bee7240fbed560d07be38`; target archive SHA-256 is `783da24d17b9ffd0dbae07903d4afb08e4bcfc575d3764c8a1c17bebda6f74d8`; the 365-file source manifest SHA-256 is `e1565ed4cc1f42c09cd4a15ef7255a749262076fec9d065f02a6b71f924cbefb`. All 16 checkout preimages matched. The port retains the existing Library activity checks, narrows the new test selector, and checks the existing read-only Library build restriction before either new scenario runner. Seven account-free source-wiring checks passed; at this candidate stage compilation and independent review were pending. Native AX, physical keyboard, installed-app and real-account behavior had not yet been exercised.

**REQ-197/198 source review:** `review: 092092c9-0e6c-447a-8984-3dc09a50ffdc PASS` for the frozen source port only. The reviewer independently checked the full 365-file manifest and archive, exact 16-path patch replay, merged PR provenance, shipping exclusion, account isolation, review-build ordering and stated Settings test boundaries. No build or test was run by the reviewer. The first local build attempt failed before compilation because macOS rejected SwiftPM's nested sandbox inside an outer network-denial sandbox. A minimal nested-versus-single sandbox probe reproduced the environment failure; its build log SHA-256 is `a5ccf5bc0fef2081325791c62351a48e56e4cc475dd92b9d5c9c5fe13c0ca2f6`. The inspected temporary root was removed. Build and execution are being separated so that the actual tests, rather than SwiftPM's nested manifest sandbox, run under network denial. This does not establish native AX, physical keyboard, installed-app or real-account acceptance.

**REQ-197/198 tested source integration:** The frozen source remained identical through two-job nonshipping and shipping builds. With networking denied during execution, all five authenticated-harness tests passed, including synthetic credential issuance, rotation, rejection and the production unified-library consumer. The actual shipping binary rejected all three test entry points and contained none of the checked test-only markers. The Settings runner's cleanup self-check passed without sending native input. Harness test log SHA-256 `6e8ddce7da46a7c7fab991510c50d46ff8970f91c8153d68cb8f4cc891b1895c`, shipping-fence log `2c2b20f460d374318656f717b9ede41bf2de837e450799591da0045c639914b3`, Settings cleanup log `19361c8ff9a897ababebfa29653f17c59c81e46e9f80f8f74e6acda6a3f8a6eb`, and shipping binary `ddfaff876348d441f215bac7633633fa025beebac5fc1300d7088689d9255fa7`. All 16 checkout preimages matched before application, all target hashes and modes matched afterward, and the checkout matched all 364 tested source paths outside this ledger. The unique test root was removed. Native Settings AX, keyboard traversal, installed-app behavior and real-account behavior remain unverified.

**REQ-197 issue boundary:** The original issue requests identifiers on each tab view and the Engine picker and Refresh control, plus native tests of tab selection, Cmd-comma, Tab focus progression and Escape. The restored runner selects the native tab selectors by visible label, checks the selected tab-view identifiers, and asserts Tab progression between the Advanced text fields. The identifiers are not claimed on the selector buttons. At this point in the record, the native scenario had not yet run.

**REQ-197 native Settings execution:** The existing native runner and the nonshipping app build ran in an isolated test mode with networking and named Keychain services and paths denied. Accessibility was already available to this runner without a permission prompt. The scenario exited 0 with nine checks: AX presses on visible Settings tab selectors switched to their identified views, the Engine picker and Refresh identifiers were present, targeted Cmd-comma opened Settings, one Tab moved from an AX-focused Advanced field to the next, and Escape dismissed Settings. The first attempt failed before launching the app because the sandbox also made the prebuilt test executable fail its `X_OK` check; the second allowed that exact session-owned executable. macOS emitted a `sandbox_extension_issue_file_to_process` warning even though the scenario returned 0. Foundation used a uniquely named `/var/folders/.../T` test bundle rather than the supplied session `TMPDIR`; its exact bundle path and matching test process were verified absent after cleanup. Receipt `req197-settings-native-v2/receipt.json` SHA-256 `aa826fe03b84050af3dbe6a641d457976c484d8d222ff7a4b39270f78c2af322` binds the inputs and logs. All 371 checkout source paths outside this ledger matched before and after. Simulated input targets only the owned test process. The receipt's `accountUsed` and `installedAppOpened` fields are declarations, not independent before-and-after preservation evidence. The allow-default sandbox and extension warning do not establish hermetic filesystem or service isolation. This does not prove keyboard traversal of the tab selectors, a complete focus cycle, physical-keyboard, VoiceOver, installed-app or other-system settings behavior.

**REQ-197 native evidence review:** `review: 5e0ee11c-36f1-4c6c-be3b-c79d98da0bd8 PASS` for the bounded source and native-test acceptance. The reviewer independently checked the receipt and every bound input and log digest, the relevant current source against the frozen manifest, the retained nine checks and cleanup of the recorded temporary bundle. The reviewer did not rerun the scenario or independently check all 371 source paths. The original issue was also read again and requests tab-view and Engine-control identifiers plus native tab switching, Cmd-comma, Tab progression and Escape.

**REQ-198 entry-point verification:** The nonshipping `XodusPreview --authenticated-scenario-runner` was recorded completing with networking denied and an isolated temporary home. It exercised synthetic credential issuance and rotation, library and subscription records, catalogue metadata, profile and gamerpic retrieval, and achievement history through the production consumers and the approved test-only transports. A separate noninteractive `--real-account-scenario-runner` invocation was recorded refusing before account work, exiting 64. An isolated bundle containing the same built executable and the real read-only Library-review metadata was recorded rejecting both scenario arguments before dispatch. Their log SHA-256 values are `d56f68874671b80caf297ecae60916ebb99b5c0f75673df1783facb94b6d029f`, `5803812f23ec9ce4a3c5e161f8ba6b86fbdbaded154061dc5db02428abbc3d6a`, and `b96203b13fc942a9dd231a9fe6bc630d772b97a3f408a04e228060cb4f890218`. The owned temporary roots were removed. The session tool records contain the commands, inline network-only sandbox and exit assertions, but did not bind the executable hash at execution time. That allow-default sandbox did not deny Keychain access; account-free behavior rests on the inspected fixture seams, not hermetic isolation. This is not live Xbox authentication or entitlement evidence.

**REQ-198 integrated-delivery review:** `review: 092092c9-0e6c-447a-8984-3dc09a50ffdc PASS` for the bounded synthetic-harness requirement. The reviewer rehashed both frozen source trees and confirmed that all 371 current non-ledger source paths match the later manifest. That run used an isolated home and denied networking, named Keychain paths and `securityd`, with further restrictions on owner-home reads, writes and process execution. The synthetic subscription record is not proof of real plan membership or a package licence.

| ID | Requirement | Acceptance | Owner | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| REQ-184 | Allow another game's install or repair to enter the download queue while a game operation is active. | Trace the disabled Install state through UI, scheduler and backend; enqueue installs and repairs in safe order without interrupting FH6, duplicating same-title work or weakening path/write conflicts; verify queued start after completion/cancellation and accurate progress/error states with regression coverage. | ff953044-fef7-4da8-939f-5e5a99f8515f (runtime abccbb57-070e-48b4-85a1-3cd3bc3e1f2b) | in-progress | Owner events `bf196a31-2796-4697-bf83-5d00f1adbd4d`, `01fc8efb-99c5-4f93-9a37-cec407348a81`, `353e795b-4396-4772-b54b-609ae93a3117`. The shipping queue uses durable `queuedInstalls` records. A separate `DownloadQueueController` has no production enqueue caller; deterministic tests reproduced early slot promotion and false cancellation there. Frozen correction tree `a8705e6cdf29d820e0248ed1d140e44892c6f1cf` passed independent source review, including inspection of the five passing focused tests and exact frozen-source identity. The reviewer did not rerun them and noted their writable test source was not independently rehashed before execution. A standalone owned `/bin/sleep` probe reproduced `Process.waitUntilExit` waiting after the child disappeared; pre-registering a termination handler joined 200 of 200 probe children. Independent cross-slice integration review passed. The separate process-join review returned REVISE: its exit assertion waited after return, task and runner cancellation could both signal the child, and a task cancelled before launch could still start it. The reviewer reproduced the first two issues in synthetic models; correction and actual-runner tests are in progress. The underlying Foundation failure mechanism and real-user impact remain unproven. This is not a live production race or installed fix. The REQ-184 queue assertions passed in the 1,297-check selector; the sole failure was a separately reproduced REQ-188 tile-readability assertion on unchanged production. The independent reviewer session was interrupted before producing a verdict; a same-session resume was requested. **REQ-184 launch-race finding, 4 November:** A separate, narrow read-only review by `ff51f5ec-5050-4d43-adcc-9ebe56698e61` confirmed that the frozen correction can still start a child after cancellation is requested. The parent checks cancellation, then awaits a detached task which calls `Process.run()` before the actor records the child. A cancellation during that interval cannot terminate the not-yet-recorded child and may allow script side effects before the parent observes it. Existing tests cover cancellation before entry and after the child publishes its PID, not this interval. This finding is source-based, not an observed live-game event, and is not a full review verdict. The frozen candidate remains uninstalled and must not be accepted as closing the launch race.

**REQ-184 revised candidate, 4 November:** The producer froze tree `8fd58dad4fc591e473a6b2a5f16850083921c2e4`, derived from `2df804129af877e63f7a6d63c4068be2662c4144`. Its shared launch owner records cancellation before a lock-protected launch commitment; cancellation accepted after commitment is retained until the child can be signaled. `Process.run()` is outside that lock because `Task.cancel()` can run its cancellation handler synchronously. A cancellation request physically arriving before OS spawn does not necessarily precede the commitment. The producer's focused real-process and queue tests report 7 tests with no failures, and the synthetic game-operation selector reports 1,297 checks with no failures. The tests directly exercise the production launch owner but do not force the entire runner into the former narrow scheduling window or stall OS spawn; no hard cancellation-latency guarantee is claimed. Archive SHA-256 `c1fb1da4690add7eac1378bc5d2da500a366cf839b9b4e5a04dffe805051a138`, manifest SHA-256 `02aa1ebe8c3c335d952df016fc54d0c2179ca8cec8a2fffaea1e9eea96ef377f`, and validation SHA-256 `ee62669b55bb626316c9cad94bd20269125265943da351c5ed3aea5163ad1905` were rechecked. Independent full-lane review followed, as recorded below. This candidate is not accepted, integrated into the user checkout, installed, or release-qualified.

**REQ-184 full-lane review, 4 November:** Reviewer `ff51f5ec-5050-4d43-adcc-9ebe56698e61` returned **REVISE** for tree `8fd58dad4fc591e473a6b2a5f16850083921c2e4`. The shared launch boundary addresses the earlier script race under its stated commitment ordering, but the composed candidate omits the installed native lifecycle's once-only cancellation guards and cancellation-error normalization. A repeated native cancel or a cancelled observation task can therefore send another termination while the child is still cleaning up. The candidate must preserve the installed behavior and cover delayed native cleanup before integration. The reviewer rechecked all archive files, the reconstructed tree and log identities but did not rerun tests. The seven reported focused tests include two script-runner tests and five queue tests using existing deterministic doubles. Neither parent exit nor a missing parent PID establishes descendant cleanup. The producer's subsequent comparison found 34 differing common paths and seven installed-only Calimocho files; not every difference is an identified defect. The new correction will start from the complete installed source lineage and overlay only the relevant script changes. The rejected candidate remains frozen and uninstalled.

**REQ-184 descendant limit:** The configured legacy install script was absent at a read-only check of `~/Library/Application Support/Xodus/Runtime/scripts/macos/private-xodus-install.sh`. Its actual child-process behavior therefore could not be inspected locally. The script-runner test's parent-process exit does not prove that descendants or other installers have stopped.

**REQ-184 install-review expiry investigation, 4 November:** The unchanged Witcher review was enabled at `10:20:46Z` and disabled by `10:26:23Z`, without confirming an install. Reopening its review ran from `10:14:44Z` to `10:14:49Z`, during which one new product-access check returned permitted at `10:14:47Z`; another product continued periodic checks. The correlation is consistent with the code's five-minute selected-title access cache, but the live log does not independently bind its anonymous event to the review's product ID. In the reviewed source, `canConfirmInstall` requires that cache, the review performs no renewal while open, and `GameInstallConsentView` observes the operations controller but not the cache's expiry notification. A stale enabled button can therefore be displayed until another update, while the confirmation guard separately rechecks access and closes a rejected review without an explanation. The review has no in-sheet access-expiry message or retry. This is a source-supported mechanism and a live state change, not proof of the original active-download failure; no queue or entitlement check has been bypassed.

**REQ-200 independent review:** `review: 86c783b2-2240-4e61-9c4a-6cc611d82d3e REVISE`. **Installed Library observation, 4 November:** The running development app showed the art-first Library at 1200 by 860 and adapted to 900 by 700; the original window size and foreground app were restored. Its accessibility tree exposed named Library, Discover and Downloads radio buttons and seven pressable `View` controls. An accessibility action selected Downloads and returned to Library without changing the foreground. This does not establish keyboard traversal, actual VoiceOver use, active-download rendering or controller-hardware behavior. Private window captures remain in session files under `req188-ocr-correction-20261104/`, with SHA-256 `195b7e0de860b8df6bdebf15931c01a10c4131db9b8d79cc9d1ca15519bd7136` and `44085e4e792e86e56589fe12cd2ddccc20f738a53cb41b352fea7da7e925140f`; they contain the owner's Library and are not release assets. The Figma MCP server reported that it lacks edit access to the selected reference, so no fresh Figma comparison is claimed.

**Earlier keyboard observation limit, 4 November:** The development app could not be activated for a real Tab traversal through either AppKit or the accessibility frontmost attribute. The subsequent macOS session read reported the screen locked and the session off-console; no keys were sent by those foreground probes. This was an environment limitation, not evidence of a keyboard defect.

**Later installed keyboard observation, 4 November:** After the Mac was on-console and unlocked, a native accessibility probe sent eight Tab events to the foreground development app. Focus moved through five distinct Library game controls, each with a nonempty `View` accessibility description; one Shift-Tab returned to the previous description. A separate position-based check also observed five distinct controls and the expected reverse step. Command-3 selected Downloads and Command-1 returned to Library, verified against the native radio-button selection values. Control-F5 did not move focus to the toolbar in this environment, whose `AppleKeyboardUIMode` was `0`; no system setting was changed. The previous foreground app was restored, and no game control was activated. Game titles were not recorded. Direct toolbar focus traversal, actual VoiceOver use, active-download rendering and controller-hardware behavior remain unverified.

**Later guarded focus observation, 4 November:** A follow-up target-card probe declined to send input because the installed app was already in the foreground. A read-only native accessibility inspection then found the focused element had role `AXSheet`; the requested enabled game control was not present in the inspected main-window tree at that moment. No key or activation was sent. The cause of the earlier target-focus mismatch remains unproven, and the sheet must not be dismissed or disturbed for this check.

**Figma access limit, 4 November:** A separate read-only metadata request for the selected Library node also reported that the Figma account lacks edit access. The file owner must grant that account editor access before a fresh direct comparison; the error is not evidence that MCP sign-in expired.

**Controller availability, 4 November:** A separate read-only GameController process reported zero connected controllers after two seconds, including a repeat after the Mac became available on-console. This does not test the installed app's controller monitor or prove that no controller is paired; physical-controller acceptance remains open.

**REQ-159/160/162 backlog triage:** [WorkingPrinciple/xode-ui#52](https://github.com/WorkingPrinciple/xode-ui/issues/52) remains open. Both current app source lineages still contain a 60-second installed-product refresh loop; the account-bound access store can renew permitted facts after two minutes or retry failures after one minute. The earlier `l8-683` branch contains a broader on-demand, shared-access and account-wide pause implementation, but it is not an ancestor of the current checkout and its affected files also contain substantial later work. A safe port must preserve Play and detail-page admission, cancellation and account boundaries and the matching backend error contract; removing the loop alone would leave current Play controls disabled after access expiry. No source was changed for this issue, and these observations do not establish that a live request was throttled.

**REQ-201 bounded source plan:** The read-only comparison by session `71b8e43b-4b71-43ed-9aff-55072a878825` recommends one shared, selected-product admission path rather than copying the unrelated historical branch. Remove automatic bulk licence triggers; reserve at most one dispatch per account and product in ten minutes, including Play, detail and Retry. Keep that budget separate from the existing five-minute fact freshness and actual provider expiry, and retain it across same-account reconnect and detail closure. If a fact expires before another dispatch is allowed, do not extend the fact or launch: explain the wait. Coalesce matching requests, recheck other queued requests at dispatch, fence account and scope changes, and preserve valid facts on failure. Cancellation must not discard another consumer's work. Backend ticket caching, typed throttling and `Retry-After`, real-account use, installation and the visible acceptance remain outside this source slice and open.

| REQ-201 evidence | Available or missing |
|---|---|
| Current access and account fencing | Existing source checks cover facts, provider expiry, account changes and late responses; their old bulk and renewal expectations must not be presented as evidence for the new policy. |
| Existing suite baseline | The unmodified isolated native selector exited 1 before its product-access checks: runtime readiness failed and an earlier catalogue-detail check ended with `nilError`. Log SHA-256 `c2d4e442561e98e08298b807414b55520167706bac752121bdf6a075d6a2272a`. It used the unchanged privacy boundary and no real account. |
| Ten-minute dispatch, shared consumers, cancellation and idle behavior | Not yet verified on the new production path. No new mock, credential substitute, replayed response or account operation is authorized. |
| Backend and visible acceptance | `Retry-After`, ticket lifetime reuse, actual account-bound transport, installed-pair behavior and the 30-minute real idle observation remain unverified. |

The red check reported retries for 408, 429, 500, 503, 504, request failure and deadline; it expected all seven to stop. Existing local toolchain and offline caches were then copied into the permitted scratch boundary; no sandbox permission was changed.

**REQ-160 ticket-reuse boundary:** On the REQ-202 backend baseline, both library and per-product licence reads call `license_tickets_typed`, which performs separate device and user exchanges. Each checked compact response has a provider lifetime, but `compact_ticket_from_response` returns only its string after validating expiry, so the caller cannot currently establish a safe reuse lifetime. Existing management-profile checks bind publication to an account/session witness and re-read the credential store; the separate legacy XSTS cache does not by itself establish the same management-profile guarantees. Reuse remains unimplemented and must preserve both ticket lifetimes, audience and account/session boundaries without exposing credentials. No provider response or account was inspected.

**REQ-160 options review:** Read-only session `3d0ec503-a7fb-4747-bd00-d21fe2c9ce45` compared no change, one management-owned in-memory device/user ticket pair, and separate component caches. It recommends the single pair, bounded by both provider expiries, the current credential/session witness, wall and monotonic time, and explicit invalidation, with the existing final publication revalidation preserved. The witness itself retains a credential-bearing fingerprint, so retention and logout/teardown eviction must be addressed before implementation; it must never be logged or persisted. Existing account-read serialization is not whole-read coalescing, and cached tickets do not establish entitlement. This is advice, not a passing implementation or proof that the provider permits ticket reuse.

**REQ-189/193 Settings readiness, 4 November:** In the installed-lineage source, the Calimocho row labels the engine `Ready` or `Installed` solely from the separate GPTK onboarding state, without consulting the Calimocho verification controller. The GPTK state itself marks a separate staging directory complete when two files exist, rather than proving the engine or game runtime is ready. This is a source finding, not an observed missing-engine event. Acceptance for the Settings row is to show Calimocho's actual verification/preparation state, avoid calling unchecked or failed engines installed or ready, distinguish the separately staged GPTK files from Calimocho's verified renderer and preserve an actionable existing recovery route. Cover the unchecked and relevant error/ready transitions without altering user engines, games or credentials.

The local XCurl checkout records `https://github.com/pendo324/xodus-xcurl` as its origin. GitHub's repository metadata reports no license, and the checked source has no license, SPDX or copyright declaration. This is not permission to copy, build into the product or redistribute that code.

The owner asked to recover the earlier FH6 setup rather than start over. App history contains a qualification-only FH6 recipe and prefix-preparation implementation at `8f714503bc4e50d820026b6f03c240f5a3eafd93`. The corresponding historical producer `fd9a4c0c28d5766b819862f8626ebc49c9616240` independently inspected the installed `11.20-4.0b3-06` engine and its exact title overlay as ready for that recipe; it rejected a different product. This is account-free preflight, not gameplay or runtime qualification. The installed producer rejects the same title alias, and the installed app lacks the title-specific loader. The old local-save bottle and the separate title-recipe prefix are absent at their recorded paths. No new prefix or game launch is authorized without resolving the owner's pending decision about the missing local saves.

The historical FH6 runtime pair has primary SHA-256 `e4c1085b4991e7af50e7e6a06245a7948d9911ae2d12b87e8fc035aacd671cb2` and legacy SHA-256 `1fb7b7c81a64c82ca2f4ea1bdc260826fe8ced3b09738e987d836eabb488b768`. Its recorded legacy source revision is `6a66c0a30f662af13a013a4aedc461b81ffdc69e`; the recovered `xstore.c` matches that revision's Git blob. Its `XStoreQueryGameLicenseAsync` sets `isActive` to true without consulting the provider, and the candidate DLL's disassembly contains that assignment. Its separate license-token path does use the provider. The historical pair therefore cannot be admitted for this task as-is: a provider-backed token elsewhere does not make the game-license response truthful. Add-on queries in this older runtime return `E_NOTIMPL`; they do not establish add-on IPC compatibility. No runtime, game, account or prefix was changed by this inspection.

An isolated REQ-192 experiment rebuilt the recovered legacy source with an explicit unsupported result when complete provider-backed game-license details are unavailable. The legacy DLL SHA-256 is `fcd00f205437bb6e45375e4f6f8a50847889119b029870c9c2920d886e7c1a0c`; the source and build-input manifest is `req192-title-recipe/runtime-legacy-build/build-manifest.json` in this session's files, SHA-256 `9b642bd34a102bc417d71643091f7b5f62c9228401d9763e740e3b374218ccb6`. The local Wine source had three tracked changes, recorded in that manifest; the full primary-runtime provenance is not established.

A real, account-free API probe used the historical primary runtime with each legacy DLL inside a separate non-game Wine prefix. With no user, the historical DLL returned success and `isActive = true`. The rebuilt DLL returned `E_NOTIMPL` from both asynchronous status and result and left the license output untouched. This proves an explicit refusal in that probe, not a provider-backed game-license implementation, current entitlement, game compatibility or gameplay. The experiment is not reviewed, installed or admitted. The user's separate fresh FH6 bottle decision remains pending; the old saves were not recovered.

Later evidence supersedes the pending-bottle and no-save-backup statements above. The owner approved a separate bottle. An archived session retained `req120/save-backup-p11b/XodusPrivateLocalSaves`, copied from the old FH6 prefix before cleanup; all 13 files still match that session's original SHA-256 manifest. The backup has not been restored, and its in-game progress and cloud interaction have not been verified. A historical graphics-settings snapshot was also recovered and copied into the separate bottle. An account-bound launch using Calimocho `0.1120.4.6` displayed the FH6 splash and driver warning, then its game process exited with code 0 before launcher cleanup. The renderer reported repeated shading-rate pipeline failures; their role in the exit is unknown. The owner reported "it started" and that the window closed. A temporary launcher was used; the installed UI path is not yet fixed.

On the next account-bound launch, a helper dismissed only the observed FH302 warning and FH6 rendered the Xbox Game Studios intro; the game later exited with code 0 before playable gameplay was observed. The same 148 shading-rate warnings occur in archived successful title-screen runs, so they do not establish the exit cause. The installed UI reported that no local Xbox runtime pair was selected, and its bundled title profiles do not contain FH6. The isolated launcher provides its own runtime and title recipe; it is not evidence that Play in the installed app works.

A subsequent run enabled the `gdkc` warning channel. FH6 called `XStoreQueryGameLicenseAsync`; the rebuilt runtime returned `E_NOTIMPL` because the provider fields needed for `XStoreGameLicense` are unavailable. The game again exited with code 0. This establishes that the unsupported API was exercised, not that it caused the exit. That channel also logged request URLs, so those URLs were redacted from the session's diagnostic logs. The owner then asked the agent to press Enter in the game.

On the owner-directed retry, a process-targeted input driver sent Enter twice after detecting the FH6 start menu. The screen advanced to "LOADING... PLEASE WAIT", then the game process exited with code 0. No gameplay was observed. The driver did not touch other applications or approve a save, cloud or account prompt. The owner also reported visible stutter; its cause and any improvement remain unverified.

The fail-closed runtime still reported the unsupported XStore capability as available. An isolated candidate now reports XStore unavailable while preserving the `E_NOTIMPL` license response; direct calls confirmed both behaviors. Its first launches were rejected by independent exact-runtime-hash checks before Wine started. A matching local launcher then passed those checks and an account-verified attempt reached "OPTIMIZING FOR YOUR PC... PLEASE WAIT" at 1% before the game exited with code 0. No XStore feature-availability query was observed; the game still called the unsupported game-license API. This does not establish why it exited or prove gameplay.

On the next comparison, the input helper sent exactly one Enter at the title screen and stopped; the game again exited with code 0. The game-process exit receipt was written before the launcher stopped its broker, and the system reported a process exit rather than a recorded forced termination. This does not identify the reason for the exit. A `PreCrashReport.xml` found in the separate bottle contains a startup snapshot, not an observed crash cause.

An account-bound, read-only provider query confirmed access and reported only field types: the FH6 grant included string `productId`, `skuId`, and `endDate`. The checked GDK active/trial/disc fields were absent. This is not sufficient by itself to manufacture a complete `XStoreGameLicense`, and it does not prove that the unimplemented API caused the exit.

Microsoft's public catalog classifies FH6 PC SKUs `0010` and `0017` as full, non-trial MSIXVC offers and `0011` as a trial. Microsoft documents disc licensing as Xbox-console-only, but does not specify complete non-trial defaults for trial ownership, duration, and unique ID. An independent research follow-up therefore found that the token and public SKU data cannot establish every native game-license field.

C-44 public broker reassessment: [Flightdeck's pinned public broker patch](https://github.com/marselnenaj/flightdeck-msfs-2024-linux-xbox/blob/c407843486b84225439f411a64f5e092fcfe501f/compat/patches/xodus-broker.patch) verifies a Microsoft-signed product grant and matches it to a full, non-trial catalog SKU and package before projecting a native game-license result. It supplies false or empty trial and disc fields for that supported case; the available Microsoft documentation does not establish that this projection is equivalent to the account-specific native result. It remains a research candidate, not an accepted license implementation. A fresh public US catalog check matched the patch's catalog conditions for SKU `0010`, but not `0017` because its catalog `IsPreOrder` field was true; `0011` was a trial. This check made no account request and does not determine the owner's SKU or entitlement.

C-44 account-binding clarification: Microsoft says the license token's `isShared` field always returns false and does not establish direct ownership; a matching entitlement ID in an appropriately authorized Collections query can link a token to the queried account. Its Collections documentation also says service queries exclude shared client entitlements, and results depend on publisher configuration. The native `isTrialOwnedByThisUser` describes the current Store user's trial entitlement, while `trialUniqueId` identifies the user and usage-limited trial. These distinctions do not establish that a catalog SKU or the broker's default trial values match the native result. Sources: [license tokens](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/gaming/gdk/docs/store/commerce/pc-specific-considerations/xstore-license-tokens?view=gdk-2604), [Collections queries](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/gaming/gdk/docs/store/commerce/service-to-service/xstore-query-user-entitlements?view=gdk-2604), [trial ownership](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/uwp/api/windows.services.store.storeapplicense.istrialownedbythisuser?view=winrt-26100), and [trial ID](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/uwp/api/windows.services.store.storeapplicense.trialuniqueid?view=winrt-26100). This was documentation research only; no new account or Collections request was made.

C-44 public runtime comparison: The game-license implementations at [nocts-software/xgameruntime revision `f1d172b`](https://github.com/nocts-software/xgameruntime/blob/f1d172b1180d45fff3e3f2b0abad517460023d0f/xstore.c) and [Chlna6666/WineGDK revision `75637b6`](https://github.com/Chlna6666/WineGDK/blob/75637b674e1f191e65753663c4c0c32bea05ba6e/dlls/xgameruntime/GDKComponent/System/XStore.c) each return an active license without consulting an account-specific entitlement in that path. Neither is an acceptable source for this missing native result. This comparison did not build, install or run either project.

C-44 native Store hypothesis: Microsoft's documented `Windows.Services.Store.StoreAppLicense` exposes the native license's account-specific active, trial, disc, expiration, and trial-identifier properties. The historical engine's account-free activation control returned `REGDB_E_CLASSNOTREG`. Check whether the admitted `11.20-4.0b3-06` release exposes the Store activation factory in a new isolated, non-game prefix; factory availability alone cannot establish an account, license result, field equivalence, or gameplay.

C-44 native Store result: On the source-bound `11.20-4.0b3-06` release, the same real account-free control successfully activated the JSON factory but received `REGDB_E_CLASSNOTREG` (`0x80040154`) for `Windows.Services.Store.StoreContext`. The probe executable SHA-256 was `6a6d7579bba5b54f67d531e595ebcc9cf5a609cffb966ad77c922dae1df50432`; its isolated Wine server was stopped and independently found absent. This rules out that factory in the tested release configuration only, not other providers or Windows installations. No game, account, license query or save was opened.

C-44 release JSON hypothesis: The admitted release's x86-64 `windows.web.dll` differs from the historical engine's file. Its JSON factory exists, but that does not establish whether JSON arrays implement the vector interface. Run the existing real array control against the release in a separate account-free prefix before deciding whether the private endpoint compatibility repair is needed there.

C-44 release JSON first observation: The real array control reported vector query status `0x00000000`, valid bounds and six successful cases when run with its historical no-vector expectation. That expectation made the control itself return nonzero, so repeat once with the observed vector expectation before accepting the result.

C-44 release JSON result: The same account-free control returned zero with `vector_expected=yes`, vector query `0x00000000`, valid empty-array bounds and six of six object-array cases. The new prefix's JSON classes pointed at its default `windows.web.dll`, whose SHA-256 `589aa8bc6a1e34ff9f3c12ec3a37aca9e1a5d3ee1ee0fc033a3b1e29fb181c15` matched the admitted release component. The prefix's Wine server was stopped and independently found absent. Thus the specific missing-vector incompatibility observed on the historical engine was not reproduced in this release configuration; this does not establish a signed-in user, license behavior or gameplay.

C-44 next action: Park unchanged FH6 relaunches and the rejected tracer, not the overall requirement. Revisit with a trustworthy source for the account-specific native license fields or a distinct, independently qualified diagnostic on an admitted engine with safe prefix ownership and complete, private evidence. The earlier corrected-JSON game already returned success from silent-user creation, so a release-only repetition of that probe would not by itself explain the later exit. The three release-engine control prefixes were confirmed idle and removed after their observations; no account, game or save was opened.

C-44 relay requalification: On the fully preflighted `11.20-4.0b3-06` release in a new account-free prefix, the revised scratch classifier observed `ExitProcess(42)` and `RtlExitUserThread(42)` with complete end-of-file streams. The existing mapper rejected a wrong Unix PID and a wrong executable name. Holding a real stream writer open produced an incomplete result with no events, rather than a positive classification. The dedicated control server exited naturally. These controls do not address launcher ownership, receipt identity, full runtime provenance, or independent review; the diagnostic remains unapproved for the game or account.

C-44 parent-lifecycle finding: Source inspection of the scratch CLI found that timeout or interruption first forwards SIGINT and then sends SIGKILL to the child's process group if it remains alive for 15 seconds. The candidate relay's signal handler alone therefore cannot guarantee that a surviving game stays open. This is a source finding, not an observed game termination. The diagnostic remains blocked for game use until the actual parent lifecycle, staged volume and broker lifetime are made consistent with C-38 and independently verified; no game was launched for this inspection.

C-44 revised launcher control: The concatenated scratch launcher ran under `/usr/bin/python3` with the admitted release engine and a real account-free control executable in a new private prefix. The control exited 42; its bound relay stream reached EOF and recorded `ExitProcess(42)` without retaining raw Wine output. A private guard socket received no connection, and the owned Wine server exited naturally. The relay receipt is `req192-title-recipe/private-release-relay-ir_jystv/evidence/exit-relay.json`, SHA-256 `3f0beb3a385d9ec82ce03ec5e23c640285ec959e028fd77c5a825f28704b7458`. Separate real-directory controls rejected a second owner, replaced prefix and replaced receipt directory, with no receipt written and no descriptor growth. These checks do not exercise the compiled parent, actual RAM staging, a game, an account, or runtime provenance. They do not address the parent-lifecycle blocker or qualify the diagnostic for FH6.

C-44 revised relay negatives: The pinned classifier was rerun on the admitted release in a fresh, account-free prefix using `req192-title-recipe/release-relay-requalification.py`, SHA-256 `025c7d077cb6d60bdc28fa16180d90cd588434c2a3d891f811ee0e4f0fe33132`. Real `ExitProcess(42)` and `RtlExitUserThread(42)` controls produced complete streams. Two simultaneous control executables with the same basename produced two mapper candidates and were rejected. A live relay stream with its final newline removed was marked incomplete with no events; a held-open writer was also incomplete with no events. Wrong PID and executable-name controls were rejected, and the server exited naturally. This validates only these account-free paths, not arbitrary traces or the compiled parent. Two earlier idle control prefixes were also checked for an owned server and open prefix files and removed; no other prefix or game was touched.

An isolated diagnostic runtime kept the same fail-closed license behavior and logged only status, not endpoints or credentials, for token/signature requests. In the account-verified run `eeb8bae9-f7d7-40f2-94a4-a624258b34e3`, one start key was sent, all 15 observed token/signature requests completed successfully, no such request failed, and the game still exited with code 0. This verifies those API calls, not the results of every downstream service or the exit cause.

C-44 connectivity-hint observation, REQ-192: A classification of that same retained run found 104 logged `XNetworkingGetConnectivityHint` refreshes with level `3` (`InternetAccess`) and cost `0` (`Unknown`). No account, game or network request was repeated. Microsoft describes the [level](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/gaming/gdk/docs/reference/networking/xnetworking/enums/xnetworkingconnectivitylevelhint?view=gdk-2604) as a best-effort hint and says it [does not establish access to a title's endpoints](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/gaming/gdk/docs/reference/networking/xnetworking/functions/xnetworkinggetconnectivityhint?view=gdk-2604). The recorded refreshes do not support an offline or local-only hint as their explanation, but do not rule out a downstream service failure or establish the exit cause.

An earlier proposed diagnostic was limited to Wine's process-exit API calls. The four-function relay filter was checked against a real Wine command in the authorized bottle and its temporary registry setting was removed afterward. The later C-44 exit-tracer review found ownership, completeness, admission and privacy blockers, so the proposed game trace remains on hold; it is not an entitlement bypass or a gameplay fix.

On the owner's request to make FH6 visible, account-verified run `fd840980-6afc-4c66-a9ab-347dda0070ec` brought the game to the foreground without a timed shutdown or automated Enter. The game remained running for over 2 minutes 26 seconds while the FH302 warning was visible. The warning then disappeared for an unknown reason; a subsequent exact-dialog helper refused to act, and the game later exited with code 0 before broker cleanup. Gameplay was not observed, and neither the warning's disappearance nor the process-exit initiator is established.

The archived installation was recorded as `Microsoft.ForteBaseGame` version `3.461.691.0`, executable SHA-256 `39b7ed4d3e5e90901b55a3dde2cc3c587c71664662616e8f8f33e188ad236db4`, about one hour before the earlier same-engine free-roam run. The current installed package is version `3.479.874.0`, executable SHA-256 `0d7a9e316691d8ae47d167311e65b46885df555cf0fcfb1ba5aa578239b5aa3d`. The earlier free-roam receipt did not record the executable hash or package version at launch, so the exact version used in that run and the role of the package change remain unproven.

Forza Support identifies `3.461.691.0` as the [5 October Windows release](https://support.forza.net/hc/en-us/articles/55985143856531-FH6-Release-Notes-October-5-2026) and `3.479.874.0` as the [2 November Windows release](https://support.forza.net/hc/en-us/articles/56954433524627-FH6-Release-Notes-November-2-2026). The new release followed the 26 October free-roam reference, but the release notes do not establish that it caused the current exit.

C-44 independent report, REQ-192: On 2 November, a Linux Xodus Nexus user reported that FH6 `3.479.874.0` began exiting silently at its title screen after updating; a second user reported the same symptom on another Linux distribution. The original reporter also described similar failures in two other Forza titles. [domsvoboda/xodus-nexus#526](https://github.com/domsvoboda/xodus-nexus/issues/526) remained open when checked on 4 November, with no confirmed cause or fix in the issue or comments. The attached public logs contain a zero exit status for the Xodus process; the [pinned launcher source](https://github.com/domsvoboda/xodus-nexus/blob/a87145e3c698b3b5d77c113d7f2e77beb308c73a/src/main/index.ts#L1298-L1330) shows this is its launched child, not independently the game process. This is a separate report on a different runtime and operating system, not a reproduction on this Mac or proof of a shared cause. It strengthens the case for investigating the current game version and shared services before repeating unchanged local launches. No game, account or save was opened for this research.

C-44 upstream distinction, REQ-192: In [domsvoboda/xodus-nexus#501](https://github.com/domsvoboda/xodus-nexus/issues/501), the reporter of an older FH6 `3.461.691.0` initialization error said an update worked, while another commenter reported the error persisted. The maintainer attributed part of that older initialization path to PlayFab SDK staging. The newer-version report in #526 already uses the recommended Nexus launcher `0.4.2`, backend `0.2.14` and runtime `76c6dd13`, yet still reports a title-screen exit. That older report does not establish a fix for the owner's current version or authorize changing save behavior.

C-44 save caution, REQ-192: The published [Forza known-issues page](https://support.forza.net/hc/en-us/articles/51701860097811-Forza-Horizon-6-Known-Issues) describes lost saves as an ongoing investigation in its 13 July 2026 list. Its conflict-dialog advice does not establish which copy is current or how this unsupported Mac runtime handles cloud synchronization. Do not select a save-conflict option or restore the historical backup without resolving those facts. This notice does not establish the cause of the title-screen exit.

C-44 license-source follow-up, REQ-192: Microsoft's [Collections publisherQuery v9 documentation](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/gaming/gdk/docs/store/commerce/service-to-service/microsoft-store-apis/xstore-v9-query-for-products?view=gdk-2604) describes SKU, entitlement dates and trial data, but states that only partner services can call it, not client apps. Its results exclude shared entitlements. Microsoft's [authentication guidance](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/gaming/gdk/docs/store/commerce/service-to-service/xstore-authenticating-your-service?view=gdk-2604) also requires the product publisher to configure the service's identity. These fields are not documented as a complete substitute for the native `XStoreGameLicense`, and this is not an authorized Ode client path. No account or provider call was made.

C-44 recipe-selection safety, REQ-192: The existing backend can silently skip a malformed higher-priority recipe and accepts a recipe whose internal Store ID differs from the requested title. Correct the existing v1 loader and launch integration to reject a present but unreadable, invalid or mismatched recipe before account, prefix or broker work; preserve ordinary launches when no recipe exists. Validate the existing schema, bound reads and retain search precedence. Exercise the real loader with the shipped FH6 edition recipe and account-free launch controls. This does not create an FH6 recipe, admit a runtime or establish gameplay.

C-44 recipe-selection candidate, REQ-192: The backend now rejects invalid, unreadable, oversized and mismatched selected recipes before engine, account, prefix and broker work. Absent recipes retain the ordinary launch path. The app reports this failure through its existing launch-result surface, without displaying the recipe path. The focused run passed 22 recipe tests, four actual account-free CLI integration tests, 21 existing lifecycle tests and two shipping UI-result tests. A separate actual CLI check used an existing Wine binary only for preflight; it did not launch Wine or access the account. Independent review is pending. This is not FH6 gameplay, a new title recipe or clean-Mac acceptance.

C-44 recipe-selection review, REQ-192: Independent reviewer `dd689a1d-2cb1-4a7a-a402-81b22d04db61` returned REVISE. All eight reviewed source hashes matched, but a rebuilt CLI cannot reach the shipping launch path with the current fixed CLI pin and associated title catalogue. The reviewer did not rerun tests. The existing account-free engine and launch-outcome check subsequently passed 207 checks. Do not repin or install an unqualified binary; the recipe slice is not accepted or shipped.

C-44 controlled-pair binding, REQ-192: Correct the existing local source-build path so its signed CLI, lifecycle pin and title-catalogue binding agree in the isolated package stage. Preserve every historical title and its evidence, keep the source-commit and producer checks, record the generated bindings, and reject inconsistent source inputs. A built pair still requires independent review and does not establish FH6 gameplay.

C-44 recipe cross-boundary control, REQ-192: The shipping-compiled `NativeGameLifecycle` invoked the real debug CLI, SHA-256 `a978e640203de046ee5fede64c6604710183b689057b6858af138682cc3416c4`, against the shipped FH6 Standard Edition recipe in an isolated directory. A mismatched base-product request produced the validated recipe-error receipt and the intended user message; with no recipe, the same path reached the existing missing-Wine error. Both stopped before prefix locks or account-service logs. The three focused UI tests passed, and a wrong CLI digest was rejected before launch. The real-CLI test explicitly skips when its three inputs are absent. It supplies an independently checked CLI identity directly to the lifecycle and therefore does not satisfy the shipping-pair admission blocker or establish gameplay. No account, Wine, game or save was opened.

C-44 cross-boundary review, REQ-192: Reviewer `dd689a1d-2cb1-4a7a-a402-81b22d04db61` inspected an earlier version of the real subprocess and receipt control, including its preflight ordering before account access. Later lower-priority recipe-location safeguards and the warning that a temporary HOME does not isolate macOS Keychain were sent for a separate follow-up; the earlier review did not cover those additions. The reviewer did not execute the control. The shipping-admission verdict remains REVISE.

C-44 staged-binding control, REQ-192: An isolated copy of the checked-out app source was rebound to the real debug CLI and compiled with shipping checks enabled. Its real bundled runtime catalogue accepted the compiled lifecycle pin and retained the historical title evidence; the real CLI recipe preflight and the other focused Swift checks also passed. Five focused source-binding checks rejected inconsistent pins, malformed or duplicate catalogue data, invalid target hashes and linked or nonregular inputs without writing staged files. This is not a signed package, shipping-pair admission, installation or gameplay. Independent reviewer `dd689a1d-2cb1-4a7a-a402-81b22d04db61` found no new concrete source defect but retained REVISE because an actual signed pair has not been admitted. The review did not execute the controls and does not qualify gameplay.

C-44 installed-lineage correction, REQ-192: The checked-out app `83036d12e2e02435afcb0a5f5a41db4fea18db07` is 23 commits behind the installed helper's recorded app source `0216d3b1220d2838109fa53e424171e9aaf721f9`. The checked-out backend `91df24b0a520283b33a656b1ef45abd86fc8bc22` diverges from installed producer `1dc05fcec7f40ba9a9b8cefe8eb24e2f1622d822`. The installed producer has no `title_recipe` module or recipe-validation references; the reproduced recipe-selection defect belongs to the other checkout, not the installed FH6 path. The earlier recipe and binding controls cannot establish a fix for the installed app. Do not build, sign or install the older candidate over the current app. Preserve the candidate and investigate the installed lineage and runtime separately.

C-44 installed Play preflight, REQ-192: The installed app's recorded source requires a primary and legacy runtime before it starts the game launcher, and its compiled-source default is nil. The installed title-profile, runtime-catalogue and candidate resources contain neither FH6 product ID. The installed app's preferences report no persisted `Xodus.lifecycle.runtime` key, consistent with the earlier observed Runtime-settings message. This establishes a missing configured runtime for the inspected path, not a missing game licence or the cause of the isolated launch's later exit. A usable runtime, truthful native licence response, required shims and game behavior remain unqualified. No account, game or save was opened.

C-44 installed shim preflight, REQ-192: The installed app's source also unconditionally validates `native-web-shims-v1` after validating the selected runtime. The directory under `~/Library/Application Support/Ode to X/Engines/profiles/` returned `ENOENT` on this Mac. If earlier admission succeeds, the missing directory is a separate blocker before the backend launches; selecting a runtime alone cannot pass this preflight. This does not establish that FH6 needs every pinned shim or explain the isolated game's later exit.

C-44 lawful shim reference, REQ-192: [BedrockOnLinux's pinned source](https://github.com/Wyze3306/BedrockOnLinux/blob/cc6ac3235bfbbf9d05fe6870b09ef71e7f1a4f52/src/xcurl-cashim.c) is covered by its stated [MIT licence](https://github.com/Wyze3306/BedrockOnLinux/blob/cc6ac3235bfbbf9d05fe6870b09ef71e7f1a4f52/LICENSE). Its adapter targets Minecraft, can log social response bodies when enabled, and rewrites a Minecraft-specific URL. Its [build script](https://github.com/Wyze3306/BedrockOnLinux/blob/cc6ac3235bfbbf9d05fe6870b09ef71e7f1a4f52/scripts/build-openssl-xcurl.sh) extracts prebuilt MSYS2 runtime DLLs. It is not a qualified FH6 replacement or a source-built dependency closure and must not be used as-is. The separate `pendo324/xodus-xcurl` source remains without an identified licence grant.

C-44 independent working reference, REQ-192: [Ferestre's pinned FH6 recipe](https://github.com/icex/ferestre/blob/2e02dd3dbc01b5e3f941c7207a8dbbf25a0163d5/titles/9NR1R1XWLCNB.toml) reports gameplay and save continuation on package `3.461.691.0`, last verified 17 October. Its [current release work](https://github.com/icex/ferestre/issues/31) reports updating to `3.479.874.0`, but explicitly excludes Forza gameplay and cloud saves from that qualification. Its [account-role runtime](https://github.com/icex/ferestre/blob/2e02dd3dbc01b5e3f941c7207a8dbbf25a0163d5/patches/xgameruntime/0030-separate-store-and-player-accounts.patch) checks the Store entitlement for `isActive`, but supplies an expiry of the current time plus 60 seconds, derives the SKU from game configuration or a placeholder, and zero-initializes the remaining fields. Its [legacy path](https://github.com/icex/ferestre/blob/2e02dd3dbc01b5e3f941c7207a8dbbf25a0163d5/patches/xgameruntime/0001-taskqueue-user-networking-gamesave-package.patch) returns an active licence unconditionally. Neither establishes the complete native licence result required here. No code or binary was imported or executed from that project, and no account or game was used.

C-44 current-version runtime comparison, REQ-192: The runtime reported in [domsvoboda/xodus-nexus#526](https://github.com/domsvoboda/xodus-nexus/issues/526) resolves through its pinned Wine source to [Nexus XGameRuntime `455b1ba9`](https://github.com/domsvoboda/nexus-xgameruntime/blob/455b1ba9c59984f9478af062fa1aec496bc58ccc/src/System/Store/GameLicense.h). Its [receipt path](https://github.com/domsvoboda/nexus-xgameruntime/blob/455b1ba9c59984f9478af062fa1aec496bc58ccc/src/System/Store/Receipts.h) verifies a Microsoft-signed token and matches the granted SKU to public catalogue data. The installed Ode producer already has a source-level bridge to request that kind of token. Nexus still zero-initializes fields not established by the grant and caps its exposed licence expiry at the proof token's expiry, so it does not establish a complete native licence result for this Mac. The two public issue logs contained none of the runtime's `store-entitled-*` result markers; they do not establish whether that user's game-license query succeeded. This is a comparison lead, not proof of a shared cause or a runtime approved for use.

C-44 upstream exit identity, REQ-192: At Nexus release `v0.4.2` commit `a87145e3c698b3b5d77c113d7f2e77beb308c73a`, the reported `The Xodus process exited with code 0` footer comes from the tracked `xodus run` backend child. Its [process wrapper](https://github.com/domsvoboda/xodus-nexus/blob/a87145e3c698b3b5d77c113d7f2e77beb308c73a/src/main/process.ts) reports the child's status and maps a missing status to `-1`. The footer is not an independent observation of the FH6 process or its reason for exiting. The public issue remains open without a confirmed shared cause or fix.

C-44 runtime privacy follow-up, REQ-192: The previously compared [`nocts-software/xgameruntime` source](https://github.com/nocts-software/xgameruntime/blob/f1d172b1180d45fff3e3f2b0abad517460023d0f/xstore.c) also logs part of a license token. It was not cloned, built or run. [Microsoft's Gaming Runtime glossary](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/gaming/gdk/docs/gdk-dev/intro/gaming-runtime-glossary) describes `xgameruntime.dll` communicating with separately installed Gaming Runtime Services. This does not establish that the official Windows components can run under Wine, or that either runtime explains FH6's exit.

The game was left running with no timed shutdown, then exited with code 0 before broker cleanup. Its Dock entry was not checked while running. Gameplay is not yet verified.

C-40 observation: Account-verified diagnostic run `7b311d53-2446-4091-ae64-e092335630c7` brought the actual game process `67192` and its FH302 warning to the foreground. The Dock contained a running item named `wine`, while two running processes shared that name; no uniquely identifiable FH6-named Dock item was observed. The exact FH302-only helper activated `Ignore Warning`; no account or save prompt was operated. A separately signed diagnostic wineserver with the installed server's identical executable text recorded the game itself requesting its own termination with code 0 at `2026-11-03T22:25:38.315602Z`; broker cleanup finished at `22:26:09.894721Z`. This identifies the Wine termination requester for this run, not the reason for the game's exit or proof that every external signal was absent. The game is not running and gameplay was not observed. The trace and `dock-observation.json` are in that run's private evidence directory.

C-40 retained-record reconciliation, REQ-192: The diagnostic server PID in the title receipt matches the trace's server PID. The trace records game PID `67192` requesting its own termination with code 0 at `22:25:38.315602Z`, followed by a nonviolent server process-close callback at `22:25:38.563779Z`. `wine-exit.json` records that same PID and return code 0; its filesystem modification time of `22:25:38.560477Z` is a receipt timestamp, not an independently timestamped process-exit event. The lifecycle events record the broker stopped cleanly with exit code 0 and no timeout at `22:26:09.890534Z`, 31.575 seconds after the self-termination request. They do not record when broker shutdown began. No retained event establishes that SIGINT or SIGTERM was sent or received, and the debugger was configured not to notify on those signals. The absence of interruption markers in the inspected logs is not proof that no signal occurred. This ordering does not establish why the game exited or rule out an earlier external trigger. The exit tracer remains parked.

C-42 license-source evidence: Microsoft documents that the PC license token's `endDate` is the license end, that disc licenses are Xbox-only, and that PC trials are supported. Its `StoreAppLicense` API exposes the native trial and license fields, but a working way to obtain that object on this Mac has not been established. Sources: [license tokens](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/gaming/gdk/docs/store/commerce/pc-specific-considerations/xstore-license-tokens?view=gdk-2604), [PC license checks](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/gaming/gdk/docs/store/commerce/pc-specific-considerations/xstore-basic-drm-and-license-checks?view=gdk-2604), [trials](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/gaming/gdk/docs/store/commerce/fundamentals/xstore-usage-limited-free-trials?view=gdk-2604) and [StoreAppLicense](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/uwp/api/windows.services.store.storeapplicense?view=winrt-28000). These facts do not supply the missing account-specific trial fields.

C-44 feasibility decision, REQ-192: Park the current exit diagnostic rather than make another speculative lifecycle patch. A one-off design that retains the stage, bridge and account broker after the spawn commitment may avoid premature cleanup, but would hold sensitive resources indefinitely and still lacks a complete account-free production-path control. A pre-spawn cancellation check alone also leaves a race; any future design needs an explicit cancellation/commit contract. Use one read-only pass over the already retained C-40 self-termination and broker records to test their ordering, then stop if the records cannot discriminate the cause. This decision does not approve a new launch or relax entitlement, staging or save safeguards.

A second account-free probe activated the real JSON array class in the same engine and confirmed its vector interface returns `0x80004002`; the selected legacy runtime requires that interface to process endpoint arrays.
