These controls do not establish FH6's exit path or license behavior.

C-44 process-identity limit: An account-free mapping probe attempted to query `ProcessWineUnixPid` (information class `1101`) against the historical reference engine. It returned `0xc0000003` (`STATUS_INVALID_INFO_CLASS`), and the source for that engine does not implement that newer Wine query. The attempted Wine-to-Unix PID mapping was therefore not established. Results are in `req192-title-recipe/wine-process-map-control-1793761289409441000/` and `req192-title-recipe/wine-process-map-control-1793761309633395000/`; the control prefix was idle after both attempts. No game or account was used.

C-44 process-identity control: A second account-free probe used the historical build's own Wine server `get_thread_times` request on a query-only thread handle. For one live known control executable, the returned Unix PID matched its host process and its Wine PID matched the filtered exit marker. A different Unix PID and a different executable basename were rejected, the control exited with its expected code 42, and the isolated prefix was idle afterward. Source, executable and runner are `req192-title-recipe/wine-process-map-v2.c`, `wine-process-map-v2.exe` and `wine-process-map-v2-control.py`, SHA-256 `2ed320ed73f7cf5a98dd1b4540141edc6ce0a53d232d4f1c6189838fe3a0b465`, `435e532ad092dccc0a8debe29266257c2eece7abcc57053576208e9a968c855e` and `df850034923e084b9dfd833815c6878082ae684e154fd7d6b523033440f35302`. The positive result and wrong-PID rejection are in `wine-process-map-control-1793761615380798000/control-result.json`, SHA-256 `a852f55358727127378a907d46cf8103a050926d8733177931971e6ea9a603a1`; the wrong-basename rejection was printed by the runner. This uses a private, version-specific Wine protocol, not the unavailable newer API. It does not qualify another Wine build, an account-bound trace or a game launch.

C-44 streaming relay control: A private memory-only classifier bound two actual, account-free Wine control processes to their Wine PIDs and observed `ExitProcess(42)` and `RtlExitUserThread(42)`, respectively. A changed mapper hash was rejected and the isolated prefix was idle afterward. In the first case the pipe did not reach EOF before the bounded reader stopped, so the positive marker is evidence but absence of other markers is not. An earlier mapping attempt timed out when started during process startup; a separate query after the target's readiness marker succeeded. The cause of the earlier timeout is not established. The revised control uses selected-server identity checking and bounded mapper waits. Its result is `req192-title-recipe/private-relay-real-control-1793762820486776000/control-result.json`, SHA-256 `c2e1cb9af694b0b3b85da98c0bc2c5762c6b7881c2b1209f5c916d11817d6a2b`. This tests the classifier directly, not the real launcher's complete output path. It does not qualify game use or establish FH6's exit cause.

C-44 launcher streaming control: The same embedded Python transport used by the scratch launcher was run with the actual system Python and a real, account-free Wine executable in a new private prefix. It bound the process, recorded `ExitProcess(42)` and the corresponding exit status, rejected an altered relay filter before launching the executable, and left the prefix idle. The captured launcher logs did not contain the control's stdout marker or the checked raw relay patterns. The stream did not reach EOF before its bounded reader stopped, so missing events are not evidence of absence. The result is `req192-title-recipe/private-relay-launch-control-1793763221026125000/control-result.json`, SHA-256 `52ddd37303e1b961aeff454435553b8b79b71ce0b8df5d1129a1a3f0f3999a70`. The first run rejected the valid filter because its Wine registry output used CRLF; a direct query established the cause and the corrected parser was used for this control. The control did not exercise the compiled CLI or an authenticated upstream socket. The candidate still requires independent privacy, process-ownership and cleanup review before any account-bound use.

C-44 diagnostic admission boundary: The isolated scratch CLI built successfully and contains the exact combined streaming scripts; its SHA-256 is `9599c94de83661e962f00a2da72eecf17fa8b296b727d68c17981adc3a54148d`. Account-free `engine-inspect` on the frozen iteration-75 registry returned `PROVIDER_NOT_ADMITTED` with this CLI, while the retained historical CLI identified the same registry as experimental and unqualified. The scratch CLI cannot be used with that historical engine under its current admission policy. Building or reviewing the diagnostic does not authorize changing or bypassing that policy, and no game or account was opened for these checks.

C-44 release-engine preflight: The same scratch CLI's account-free `title-recipe-inspect` returned ready for the existing `calimocho-06-fh6-v1` registry, SHA-256 `ba3ad1be519040c04abf7a55d99e3ec67038913490b577cbeed74c039110f597`, using the source-bound `11.20-4.0b3-06` release. This inspection checks the title and engine profile, not a selected Xbox runtime, account, installed UI or gameplay. The scratch tracer remains tied to the different historical engine and is not approved for either engine.

C-44 exit-tracer review: Independent reviewer `8029ea76-69cf-416e-9d50-07a303349633` returned REVISE for the private scratch tracer. The review found that prefix ownership is not held exclusively through server cleanup; incomplete relay streams can still produce positive classifications without a completeness gate; and the hardcoded historical engine has neither current provider admission nor a complete build binding. It also found a receipt-directory time-of-check/time-of-use gap and that the output test persists logs while checking only selected markers. The diagnostic remains unapproved for an account or game. No engine-admission exception is authorized.

C-44 retained-log classification: The two corrected-JSON game runs each recorded two `0x000006ba` exceptions in the existing diagnostic, one through COM and one through WinHTTP and the Xbox runtime. The diagnostic records them before structured exception handlers run, so they are not evidence of an unhandled exception or the cause of the exit. In the later run, token completion was observed after those records. No new game launch or account request was made for this classification.

C-44 progress comparison: In the retained iteration-65 gameplay trace, two logged `XUserAddAsync` calls both used the silent option `0x1`; the second call and result occurred after the first 14 PlayFab records and before the later PlayFab login records. The recent user-traced game recorded one silent add and result and only the initial 14 PlayFab records. This is a difference in observed call sequence, not proof that the second add caused the later progress or that the game received its title-screen input. The game version, prefix, runtime and license behavior also differ. This comparison used fixed markers and numeric options only, without a new launch or account request.

C-44 JSON compatibility slice, REQ-192: Evaluate a private runtime fallback only when the actual JSON array returns `E_NOINTERFACE` for its vector interface. The fallback must enumerate real object values, distinguish `E_BOUNDS` from malformed elements, preserve other failures, and retain the existing vector path. Verify both paths against actual Wine JSON implementations and then, if those controls pass, a noninteractive signed-in user probe with the original JSON implementation. This slice does not establish licensing, game startup, runtime admission or gameplay.

C-44 JSON compatibility evidence: An isolated private-source candidate uses `IJsonArray_GetObjectAt` only after vector lookup returns `E_NOINTERFACE`. Before the change, six real-array checks failed on the historical builtin JSON implementation with `0x80004002`; after the change, all six passed on that implementation and on the newer native vector implementation, including empty arrays, multiple objects and malformed elements. The control independently checked the expected vector status, the loader type and `E_BOUNDS`. One noninteractive probe then used the original builtin JSON implementation and the candidate runtime, observed two fixed candidate markers, completed one broker request, returned `S_OK` for silent-user creation and state, and reported signed in. The account matched before and after and the probe prefix was idle afterward. Independent source review `ad64ef9c-e045-44b3-a871-2617b82d47d1` returned PASS for this diagnostic slice only; it did not rerun the controls or verify the binary binding. The parent separately checked both changed source files and the binary against `req192-title-recipe/json-array-runtime-control/runtime-legacy-build3/build-manifest.json`, SHA-256 `63e01bccbc3d4a9eb6ae5cbd864ddb96998f5f536a84f38675c5776b73f5ce0a`. Exceptional operational errors and the overflow limit were reviewed in source, not exercised. The private candidate is not admitted, packaged or a game-playability fix.

C-44 repository port, REQ-196: The private backend now uses the same reviewed helper in both endpoint-array size calls, without the scratch diagnostic marker or any change to the separate license parser. The actual repository WinRT test, compiled with a separately extracted vector-only baseline, failed all six cases against the historical builtin JSON implementation. With the repository helper it passed all six cases against both the historical builtin and newer native implementations; vector status, bounds and pointer checks also passed. The existing endpoint-policy regression passed. The private legacy source was built in session scratch from repository HEAD `91df24b0a520283b33a656b1ef45abd86fc8bc22` plus the uncommitted port. Its separate manifest binds 93 source files and 204 compiler dependency files; manifest SHA-256 `5d5521a022037f447d43e1d2e2e7da9ba07d17de731fadf5687454f4e0a6f21d`, DLL SHA-256 `4f91841f744de9e65d09152259577d4ec2ac931195f10a545511d5f1b4e8aeb3`. The historical source manifest remains unchanged. Independent review `ad64ef9c-e045-44b3-a871-2617b82d47d1` returned PASS for this repository port and its bound evidence. Arbitrary COM operational failures, success-with-null, the helper's overflow limit and allocation failure were reviewed in source, not exercised; the existing policy fixture covers only the vector path. The test runner cannot establish prefix isolation, idleness or matching Wine inputs; those remain caller preconditions. This source build was not exercised with an account or game, and the previous authenticated result applies only to the scratch candidate.

C-44 input comparison: In the earlier run `c35ae056-e876-4d79-ae92-3fb252480d0c`, the retained macOS menu driver posted two process-targeted Enter events with an 80 ms key hold, and a loading screen was observed. The later guarded observer used a different input path: its retained source posts key down and key up consecutively to the HID event stream, with no explicit hold. That later run recorded a post, not receipt by FH6, and the title screen remained in the final capture. The changed input route, duration and number of keys are possible confounders, not evidence that input or licensing caused the exit. This comparison used existing source and receipts only; no new game or account operation was started.

C-44 input-path control: In the isolated, account-free reference-engine prefix, the exact host process was mapped to its Wine PID and a real blank Wine Notepad window received one process-targeted Return held for 80 ms. Its edit control changed from zero characters and one line to exactly one CRLF and two lines. No game or account was used, and the control prefix was idle afterward. The runner is `req192-title-recipe/wine-notepad-return-control.py`, SHA-256 `a0fc957f34fd7559dea4322ff7803e8d88e7d1207773453d38e450b3437e052d`; its result is `wine-notepad-return-control-1793761955307428000/control-result.json`, SHA-256 `1c8e247902804c7b6276e72d61e4953ea7f40f55195c1b91c20456a8df86cff5`. This qualifies delivery to Notepad only. FH6 may use a different input path, and the control does not explain its exit or establish gameplay.

C-44 title-guard availability: The existing accurate Vision OCR guard and an independent candidate both failed when reading a retained title frame. A separate account-free check classified the error as `TextRecognition.CRImageReaderError`, code 1, with an `e5rt` execution-stream failure. Accurate OCR also failed when restricted to CPU, with supported revisions 1 and 2, and after the frame was downscaled to maximum dimensions of 1024 and 2048 pixels. The same accurate error occurred on an unrelated existing Ode screenshot in a fresh process, while fast OCR returned observations for both images. This shows the failure is not specific to the retained game frame; its cause is not established. The fast OCR path recognized the retained title frame, but its ability to reject protected account or save prompts has not been established. No game was started and no input was sent for these checks. Do not substitute the fast path for the protected-input guard without evidence that it is safe.

C-44 save-provider boundary: Microsoft PlayFabCSdk revision `c2c0321c5535124e25c59bf218f3653eb2878f32` selects its in-process Win32 game-save provider when the `ForceUseInprocGameSaves` registry value is set; its [provider source](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/PlayFab/PlayFabCSdk/c2c0321c5535124e25c59bf218f3653eb2878f32/Source/PlayFabGameSave/Source/Platform/GDK/Platform_GDK.cpp) does not establish that the user's game contains this revision. Microsoft's [sample](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/PlayFab/PlayFabCSdk/c2c0321c5535124e25c59bf218f3653eb2878f32/Samples/PlayFabGameSaveSample-Windows/GameSaveIntegration.cpp) says the registry override is for testing and not production. The [in-process provider](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/PlayFab/PlayFabCSdk/c2c0321c5535124e25c59bf218f3653eb2878f32/Source/PlayFabGameSave/Source/Platform/Windows/PFGameSaveFilesAPIProvider_Win32.cpp) includes cloud download, upload and reset paths. This switch is not evidence that cloud synchronization, conflicts or existing saves are safe. No save-provider setting, account or game was changed.

C-44 persistent-storage prerequisite, REQ-192: The private legacy runtime's `XPersistentLocalStorageGetPathSize` and `XPersistentLocalStorageGetPath` are unimplemented. A [public macOS FH6 report](https://github.com/domsvoboda/xodus-nexus/issues/228) describes an older game version reaching play after a separate save adapter was staged; that adapter requires these APIs when the SDK supplies no save folder. Implement and independently review only a stable, manifest-title-specific persistent storage path inside a private Wine prefix, with explicit missing-identity, invalid-path and buffer failures. Exercise the real compiled runtime in an account-free isolated prefix, including repeat calls, distinct title identities and directory safety. Do not stage the adapter, alter existing game or save files, select a save, or claim that this prerequisite explains the current game's exit or qualifies cloud synchronization.

C-44 persistent-storage baseline, REQ-192: The real storage interface in the independently reviewed private runtime build returned `E_NOTIMPL` for the size call, including after a valid test title identity was initialized. Five expected API behaviors failed in the account-free control; the loaded DLL matched the recorded runtime SHA-256 `4f91841f744de9e65d09152259577d4ec2ac931195f10a545511d5f1b4e8aeb3`. The fixed-status log is `req192-title-recipe/persistent-storage-control/baseline-debug.log`, SHA-256 `581e413f8549fdd38e264800aeb2f78cca6444a0eb62f72091c84c5180c0744d`. Its session-owned Wine server was stopped, and no account, game or existing save was opened.

C-44 persistent-storage candidate, REQ-192: A private, unshipped source candidate derives a UTF-8 path from the validated manifest title ID, keeps it separate from the existing save provider and returns explicit errors for missing identity, invalid paths and short buffers. Eight real-Wine scenarios passed 213 checks, including repeat activations, distinct title IDs, Unicode paths, existing-provider setup, a regular-file obstruction and a symbolic-link obstruction. The control used an account-free test prefix whose `C:` drive mapped to its own `drive_c`, and its Wine server and observed service processes were stopped afterward. Candidate DLL SHA-256 `893a64c4be2d5f0aa83e348cb13b2012895bb6d5a7b0e4c555088b51f16a523b`; source/build manifest SHA-256 `5b12f75ff7a3e49abd31b9014992169ec42445ce33b236024d83ea669e70459b`; control log SHA-256 `934f5818d1dda649f07f05d692a1a0f299e0a171a0a5edd16aa769fc4b39baf6`. The existing local-save quota and endpoint-policy checks also passed. Independent review returned REVISE: production `C:` mapping to the private prefix is not verified; path-based reparse-point checks are vulnerable to concurrent replacement before use; and filesystem errors are collapsed to `ERROR_DIRECTORY`. The test verifies mapping only in its isolated test prefix and static file/link obstructions; it does not exercise concurrent replacement or distinct filesystem errors. Do not deploy it or stage an adapter on this evidence. No account, game or existing save was opened.

C-44 native-license availability: A real, account-free WinRT activation control in an isolated prefix used the historical reference engine. Its JSON factory positive control succeeded, while `Windows.Services.Store.StoreContext` returned `0x80040154` (`REGDB_E_CLASSNOTREG`) with no factory. The prefix was idle afterward. The control source, executable and fixed-status log are in `req192-title-recipe/json-array-runtime-control/`; their SHA-256 values are `a565a2d502f7f450675a1106fdb40273c9b577726a34bb1bf837e64234c67be7`, `6a6d7579bba5b54f67d531e595ebcc9cf5a609cffb966ad77c922dae1df50432` and `d77b2bfa4d4530b6cd2587e0a56e62b8db5796fa531cb29694880371201e659e`, respectively. This confirms that class is unavailable in that isolated configuration; it does not rule out a supported implementation elsewhere or prove the cause of the game's exit.

## Handoff

REQ-192/194: FH6 still exits at the title screen, and its cause is unknown. The recipe-safety candidate remains unshipped because the rebuilt CLI is not admitted by the fixed shipping pair. Wait for a new discriminating observation or a qualified diagnostic; do not repeat an unchanged game launch, invent native license fields, or alter the saved game. Repeat-launch, save, performance and clean-Mac acceptance remain open.

Next: The signed v0.2.0 assets are public. The original assets without Developer ID signatures and their checksum-only release notes remain visible. Approval to clarify the public notes was requested, but the owner was unavailable; this is not approval to edit them. Exact backend source-to-binary provenance is not established by the surviving output match alone. The five v0.2.1 release-preparation commits are preserved on the existing GitHub branch `dragoshont-app-harness-and-packaging` at `08a99a27d6350fce54adda36c12c547f0bc13b7e`; they do not constitute a v0.2.1 release.

Failed approaches: The initial scope declaration rejected this ledger because it lacked a handoff. The original public app's helper receipt recorded the pre-signing helper hash, so its bundled helper failed admission. The isolated signed copy records the signed helper hash and passes the real anonymous management handshake.

Verify: Download and checksum any published assets.

Same fingerprint three times: unchanged relaunches stopped. Cause unknown; game package 3.479.874.0 vs historical 3.461.691.0 remains an unproven confounder.
